the Sosa, Plitt & Giocomo (2025) hippocampal CA1 2P + VR dataset\n(DANDI:001361, NWB) into the decoder-ready pickle format.\n\nUsage:\n    python -u /app/convert_data.py <outpicklefile> [--full | --sample] [--show-processing]\n\nOptions:\n    --full              process all sessions (default)\n    --sample            process only 2 sessions (for testing)\n    --show-processing   save per-step diagnostic plots for up to 2 sessions\n                        as processing_<session_id>.png\n\nProcessing follows the reference repository (`/app/code/src/reward_relative`) and\nthe paper methods (`/app/methods.txt`):\n\n  * neural signal  : raw suite2p F/Fneu  ->  dF/F (per-trial maximin baseline,\n                     neuropil subtraction with coefficient 0.7, sigma=2-sample\n                     Gaussian smoothing), exactly as\n                     `reward_relative.preprocessing.dff(...,\n                     neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin')` is\n                     called from `reward_relative.utilities.multi_anim_sess`.\n                     dF/F is the default neural signal written to the pickle;\n                     `--neural-signal events` instead writes the OASIS\n                     deconvolution of that dF/F (the reference `deconvolve=True`\n                     branch, used for the paper's own decoder).\n  * neuron curation: suite2p `iscell` (manual curation, stored in the NWB\n                     PlaneSegmentation) then putative-interneuron exclusion\n                     (Pearson r(dF/F, speed) > 0.5), as in\n                     `reward_relative.spatial.is_putative_interneuron`.\n  * trials         : [trial_start_ind, teleport_ind)  (the teleport sample is\n                     excluded; its interpolated position is meaningless).\n  * trial curation : trials with lick-sensor error (>30% of frames with a\n                     cumulative lick count > 2) are dropped\n                     (`reward_relative.behavior.correct_lick_sensor_error`).\n  * reward zones   : from the scene name in the NWB identifier, switching at\n                     trial 30 (`reward_relative.behavior.get_reward_zones`).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport glob\nimport multiprocessing\nimport os\nimport pickle\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport warnings\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import ndimage\n\nfrom pynwb import NWBHDF5IO\nfrom suite2p.extraction import dcnv\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Constants taken from the reference code / paper methods\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# reward_relative.behavior.reward_zone_dict; get_reward_zones maps scene\n# 'Location A/B/C' -> dict keys 'X'/'Y'/'Z' (paper: zone A 80-130, B 200-250,\n# C 320-370 cm)\nREWARD_ZONE_CM = {'A': (80.0, 130.0), 'B': (200.0, 250.0), 'C': (320.0, 370.0)}\nRZ_LABELS = ['A', 'B', 'C']\nCHANGE_TRIAL = 30           # reward zone switches after 30 trials on switch days\n\nNEU_COEF = 0.7              # neuropil coefficient (default_dff_method)\nMAXIMIN_WIN = 300           # samples (~20 s at 15.5 Hz), from preprocessing.dff\nBASELINE_SMOOTH_SIG = 15    # samples, from preprocessing.dff  (nansmooth [0,15])\nDFF_SMOOTH_SIG = 2          # samples (~0.129 s), from preprocessing.dff\nTAU = 0.7                   # suite2p ops['tau'] used by the authors\nOASIS_BATCH = 2000\nINT_R_THRESH = 0.5          # dayData.int_thresh, putative interneuron cutoff\nLICK_ERROR_THRESH = 0.3     # paper: \">30% of samples with cumulative lick count >2\"\n\nTRACK_LENGTH = 450.0\n\n# reward_relative.teleport_metadata.teleport_sessions: experiment days on which\n# the laser was NOT blanked during the inter-trial interval, so the ITI\n# fluorescence is usable for the per-trial dF/F baseline.\nTELEPORT_SESSIONS = {\n    'm10': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15],\n    'm11': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15],\n    'm12': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15],\n    'm13': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15],\n    'm14': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15],\n    'm15': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17],\n    'm17': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17],\n    'm18': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17],\n    'm19': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17],\n}\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_trial_start_s', 'environment', 'trial_number',\n               'previous_trial_outcome']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['distance_to_reward_zone', 'position', 'speed', 'lick',\n                'reward_zone_location', 'reward_outcome']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['< -50 cm', '-50 to -10 cm', '-10 to 0 cm', 'in reward zone (0 cm)',\n     '0 to +10 cm', '+10 to +50 cm', '> +50 cm'],\n    ['< 90 cm', '90-180 cm', '180-270 cm', '270-360 cm', '> 360 cm'],\n    ['< 2 cm/s', '2-10 cm/s', '10-20 cm/s', '20-40 cm/s', '> 40 cm/s'],\n    ['no lick', 'lick'],\n    ['zone A (80-130 cm)', 'zone B (200-250 cm)', 'zone C (320-370 cm)'],\n    ['omitted', 'rewarded'],\n]\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Helpers copied / adapted from the reference repo\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=-1):\n    \"\"\"Gaussian smoothing that does not propagate NaNs.\n\n    Port of ``TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth`` / ``reward_relative.utilities.nansmooth``\n    (TwoPUtils is not installed here). A list ``sig`` applies an N-d Gaussian\n    filter (as the reference does with ``[0, 15]``); a scalar ``sig`` applies a\n    1-d filter along ``axis``.\n    \"\"\"\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.array(a, dtype=np.float64, copy=True)\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    if np.isscalar(sig):\n        a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis)\n        one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\n    else:\n        a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig)\n        one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig)\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef scene_reward_zones(scene, n_trials):\n    \"\"\"Reward-zone [start, stop] coordinates and labels per trial, from the scene name.\n\n    Port of ``reward_relative.behavior.get_reward_zones`` restricted to the\n    scenes that occur in this dataset (Env{1,2}_Location{A,B,C} and the\n    within-session switch scenes ``*_X_to_*_Y``). The zone changes after\n    ``CHANGE_TRIAL`` (=30) trials on switch days.\n    \"\"\"\n    def _zone_of(label):\n        return REWARD_ZONE_CM[label]\n\n    # Scenes are like 'Env1_LocationA', 'Env1_LocationA_to_C', 'Env1_A_to_Env2_B'\n    if '_to_' in scene:\n        # first zone: last letter of the token before '_to_'\n        before, after = scene.split('_to_')\n        first = before[-1]\n        second = after[-1]\n        assert first in REWARD_ZONE_CM and second in REWARD_ZONE_CM, scene\n        labels = np.array([first] * min(CHANGE_TRIAL, n_trials) +\n                          [second] * max(0, n_trials - CHANGE_TRIAL))\n    else:\n        lab = scene[-1]\n        assert lab in REWARD_ZONE_CM, scene\n        labels = np.array([lab] * n_trials)\n    coords = np.array([_zone_of(l) for l in labels], dtype=np.float64)\n    return coords, labels\n\n\ndef compute_dff_events(F, Fneu, windows, fs, deconvolve=True):\n    \"\"\"dF/F and OASIS-deconvolved 'events', following ``preprocessing.dff``.\n\n    Args:\n        F: (ncells, nframes) raw suite2p fluorescence\n        Fneu: (ncells, nframes) neuropil fluorescence\n        windows: list of (start, stop) sample indices defining the periods over\n            which dF/F is computed (one per trial; includes the preceding ITI\n            when the laser was not blanked)\n        fs: imaging rate per plane, Hz\n        deconvolve: whether to also run the OASIS deconvolution\n\n    Returns:\n        dff, events: (ncells, nframes) float32 arrays, NaN outside `windows`\n        (events is None when deconvolve is False)\n    \"\"\"\n    f_ = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    fneu_ = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for s, e in windows:\n        f_[:, s:e] = F[:, s:e]\n        fneu_[:, s:e] = Fneu[:, s:e]\n\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    # neuropil subtraction\n    f_ -= NEU_COEF * fneu_\n\n    flow = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    for s, e in windows:\n        # add back the neuropil mean within the window so dF/F values stay close\n        # to true dF/F (reference comment)\n        f_[:, s:e] += NEU_COEF * np.nanmean(fneu_[:, s:e], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        # maximin baseline: smooth, then 20 s minimum filter, then 20 s maximum filter\n        x = nansmooth(f_[:, s:e], [0, BASELINE_SMOOTH_SIG])\n        x = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(x, MAXIMIN_WIN, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, s:e] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(x, MAXIMIN_WIN, axis=-1)\n\n    dff = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = ((f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask])\n                       / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask]))\n\n    events = np.full(F.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32) if deconvolve else None\n    for s, e in windows:\n        dff[:, s:e] = nansmooth(dff[:, s:e], DFF_SMOOTH_SIG, axis=1)\n        if deconvolve:\n            events[:, s:e] = dcnv.oasis(np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, s:e]),\n                                        OASIS_BATCH, TAU, fs)\n    return dff, events, nanmask\n\n\ndef speed_correlation(dff, speed, nanmask):\n    \"\"\"Pearson r between each cell's dF/F and running speed over valid samples.\n\n    Vectorised equivalent of ``reward_relative.spatial.is_putative_interneuron``\n    with ``method='speed'``.\n    \"\"\"\n    D = dff[:, nanmask].astype(np.float64)\n    S = np.asarray(speed, dtype=np.float64)[nanmask]\n    Dm = D - D.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    Sm = S - S.mean()\n    denom = np.sqrt((Dm ** 2).sum(axis=1)) * np.sqrt((Sm ** 2).sum())\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):\n        r = (Dm @ Sm) / denom\n    return r\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Discretisation of the decoder outputs\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef discretize_reward_distance(d):\n    \"\"\"Signed distance (cm) to the nearest point of the reward zone -> 7 bins.\"\"\"\n    out = np.empty(d.shape, dtype=np.int64)\n    out[:] = 3                                  # d == 0: inside the reward zone\n    out[(d >= -10) & (d < 0)] = 2\n    out[(d >= -50) & (d < -10)] = 1\n    out[d < -50] = 0\n    out[(d > 0) & (d <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(d > 10) & (d <= 50)] = 5\n    out[d > 50] = 6\n    return out\n\n\ndef discretize_position(pos):\n    \"\"\"Absolute track position (cm) -> 5 equal bins spanning the 450 cm track.\"\"\"\n    return np.clip((pos / (TRACK_LENGTH / 5)).astype(np.int64), 0, 4)\n\n\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Running speed (cm/s) -> 5 bins.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(speed, [2.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0]).astype(np.int64)\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef load_session(path):\n    \"\"\"Read everything needed for one session out of the NWB file with pynwb.\"\"\"\n    io = NWBHDF5IO(path, 'r', load_namespaces=True)\n    nwb = io.read()\n\n    subject = nwb.subject.subject_id\n    exp_day = int(nwb.session_id)\n    scene = nwb.identifier.rstrip('/').split('/')[-1]\n    date = nwb.identifier.rstrip('/').split('/')[-2]\n\n    beh = nwb.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']\n    get = lambda k: np.asarray(beh[k].data[:], dtype=np.float64)\n    frame_times = np.asarray(beh['position'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n    nframes = len(frame_times)\n\n    behavior = dict(\n        time=frame_times,\n        pos=get('position'),\n        speed=get('speed'),\n        lick=get('lick'),\n        rzone=get('reward_zone'),\n        trial_start=get('trial_start'),\n        teleport=get('teleport'),\n        trialnum=get('trial number'),\n        morph=get('environment'),\n        autoreward=get('autoreward'),\n    )\n    # 'Reward' is a sparse series: one timestamp per delivered reward. Convert to\n    # a per-frame binary, equivalent to the vr_data['reward'] column.\n    reward_times = np.asarray(beh['Reward'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n    reward_frames = np.searchsorted(frame_times, reward_times)\n    reward_frames = np.clip(reward_frames, 0, nframes - 1)\n    reward = np.zeros(nframes)\n    np.add.at(reward, reward_frames, 1.0)\n    behavior['reward'] = reward\n\n    # --- ophys: raw F and neuropil for suite2p-curated cells, all planes pooled\n    ophys = nwb.processing['ophys']\n    seg = ophys['ImageSegmentation']['PlaneSegmentation']\n    iscell = np.asarray(seg['iscell'].data)[:, 0] > 0\n    plane_idx = np.asarray(seg['planeIdx'].data).astype(int)\n\n    F_list, Fneu_list, plane_list = [], [], []\n    rate = None\n    for key in sorted(ophys['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series.keys()):\n        p = int(key.replace('plane', ''))\n        mask = iscell[plane_idx == p]\n        rrs = ophys['Fluorescence'][key]\n        rate = rrs.rate\n        # NWB stores (nframes, nroi); the reference works with (ncells, nframes).\n        # Some sessions have exactly one more imaging frame than behaviour rows\n        # (the \"one frame correction\" in vr_align_to_2P) -> truncate.\n        F_list.append(np.asarray(rrs.data[:nframes, :])[:, mask].T.astype(np.float32))\n        Fneu_list.append(np.asarray(ophys['Neuropil'][key].data[:nframes, :])[:, mask].T\n                         .astype(np.float32))\n        plane_list.append(np.full(int(mask.sum()), p, dtype=int))\n\n    F = np.concatenate(F_list, axis=0)\n    Fneu = np.concatenate(Fneu_list, axis=0)\n    planes = np.concatenate(plane_list, axis=0)\n    n_planes = len(F_list)\n    io.close()\n\n    return dict(path=path, subject=subject, exp_day=exp_day, scene=scene, date=date,\n                behavior=behavior, F=F, Fneu=Fneu, planes=planes,\n                n_planes=n_planes, rate=rate, nframes=nframes)\n\n\ndef convert_session(path, show_processing=False, plot_dir='/app', neural_signal='dff'):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB session into per-trial neural / input / output arrays.\"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    sess = load_session(path)\n    t_load = time.time() - t0\n\n    beh = sess['behavior']\n    nframes = sess['nframes']\n    fs = sess['rate'] / sess['n_planes']          # per-plane imaging rate (Hz)\n\n    tstart_inds = np.where(beh['trial_start'] > 0)[0]\n    teleport_inds = np.where(beh['teleport'] > 0)[0]\n    assert len(tstart_inds) == len(teleport_inds), \\\n        f\"{path}: {len(tstart_inds)} trial starts vs {len(teleport_inds)} teleports\"\n    assert np.all(teleport_inds > tstart_inds), f\"{path}: teleport before trial start\"\n    n_trials_raw = len(tstart_inds)\n\n    # --- dF/F windows (reference `dff`): per trial, optionally including the\n    # preceding ITI on sessions where the laser was not blanked.\n    keep_teleports = sess['exp_day'] in TELEPORT_SESSIONS.get(sess['subject'], [])\n    if keep_teleports:\n        windows = [(int(tstart_inds[0]), int(teleport_inds[0]))]\n        for i in range(1, n_trials_raw):\n            # start 1 sample after the previous teleport (the teleport sample\n            # itself is excluded, as in the reference)\n            windows.append((int(min(teleport_inds[i - 1] + 1, tstart_inds[i])),\n                            int(teleport_inds[i])))\n    else:\n        windows = [(int(s), int(e)) for s, e in zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds)]\n\n    t1 = time.time()\n    dff, events, nanmask = compute_dff_events(sess['F'], sess['Fneu'], windows, fs,\n                                              deconvolve=(neural_signal == 'events'\n                                                          or show_processing))\n    t_dff = time.time() - t1\n\n    # --- neuron curation: putative interneurons (speed-correlated) ------------\n    r_speed = speed_correlation(dff, beh['speed'], nanmask)\n    is_int = np.nan_to_num(r_speed, nan=0.0) > INT_R_THRESH\n    keep_cells = ~is_int\n    n_cells_iscell = len(keep_cells)\n    neural_full = (events if neural_signal == 'events' else dff)[keep_cells]\n    planes = sess['planes'][keep_cells]\n\n    # --- per-trial behaviour --------------------------------------------------\n    rz_coords, rz_labels = scene_reward_zones(sess['scene'], n_trials_raw)\n\n    # reward_relative.behavior.get_trial_types: rewarded iff reward delivered AND\n    # the reward-zone flag was set within the trial\n    isreward = np.zeros(n_trials_raw, dtype=np.int64)\n    morph = np.zeros(n_trials_raw, dtype=np.int64)\n    trialnum = np.zeros(n_trials_raw, dtype=np.int64)\n    lick_error = np.zeros(n_trials_raw, dtype=bool)\n    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(zip(tstart_inds, teleport_inds)):\n        isreward[i] = int(np.any(beh['reward'][s:e] > 0)\n                          and np.any(beh['rzone'][s:e] > 0))\n        m = np.unique(beh['morph'][s:e])\n        morph[i] = int(np.round(m[0]))\n        tn = np.unique(beh['trialnum'][s:e])\n        trialnum[i] = int(tn[0])\n        L = beh['lick'][s:e]\n        lick_error[i] = np.mean(L > 2) > LICK_ERROR_THRESH\n\n    # previous trial outcome (0 = omitted, 1 = rewarded); undefined (=0) for the\n    # first trial of a session\n    prev_outcome = np.concatenate([[0], isreward[:-1]]).astype(np.int64)\n\n    # --- build per-trial arrays ----------------------------------------------\n    neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials = [], [], []\n    kept_trial_idx = []\n    n_dropped_lick, n_dropped_nan = 0, 0\n    for i in range(n_trials_raw):\n        if lick_error[i]:\n            n_dropped_lick += 1\n            continue\n        s, e = int(tstart_inds[i]), int(teleport_inds[i])\n        pos = beh['pos'][s:e]\n        speed = beh['speed'][s:e]\n        lick = beh['lick'][s:e]\n        t = beh['time'][s:e] - beh['time'][s]\n        neural = neural_full[:, s:e]\n\n        if (not np.all(np.isfinite(neural)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(pos))\n                or not np.all(np.isfinite(speed)) or not np.all(np.isfinite(lick))):\n            n_dropped_nan += 1\n            continue\n\n        rz_start, rz_end = rz_coords[i]\n        d = np.zeros_like(pos)\n        d[pos < rz_start] = pos[pos < rz_start] - rz_start\n        d[pos > rz_end] = pos[pos > rz_end] - rz_end\n\n        out = np.stack([\n            discretize_reward_distance(d),\n            discretize_position(pos),\n            discretize_speed(speed),\n            (lick > 0).astype(np.int64),\n            np.full(len(pos), RZ_LABELS.index(rz_labels[i]), dtype=np.int64),\n            np.full(len(pos), isreward[i], dtype=np.int64),\n        ], axis=0)\n\n        inp = np.stack([\n            t,\n            np.full(len(pos), float(morph[i])),\n            np.full(len(pos), float(trialnum[i])),\n            np.full(len(pos), float(prev_outcome[i])),\n        ], axis=0).astype(np.float32)\n\n        neural_trials.append(np.ascontiguousarray(neural, dtype=np.float32))\n        input_trials.append(inp)\n        output_trials.append(out)\n        kept_trial_idx.append(i)\n\n    info = dict(\n        path=path, subject=sess['subject'], exp_day=sess['exp_day'],\n        scene=sess['scene'], date=sess['date'],\n        session_id=f\"{sess['subject']}_day{sess['exp_day']:02d}\",\n        n_trials_raw=n_trials_raw, n_trials_kept=len(neural_trials),\n        n_dropped_lick_error=n_dropped_lick, n_dropped_nan=n_dropped_nan,\n        n_rois_iscell=n_cells_iscell, n_interneurons=int(is_int.sum()),\n        n_cells=int(keep_cells.sum()), n_planes=sess['n_planes'],\n        keep_teleports=bool(keep_teleports), fs=float(fs),\n        frac_rewarded=float(np.mean(isreward)),\n        rz_labels=list(rz_labels), morph=morph.tolist(),\n        t_load=t_load, t_dff=t_dff,\n    )\n\n    if show_processing:\n        plot_processing(sess, beh, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, dff, events,\n                        r_speed, keep_cells, rz_coords, rz_labels, isreward,\n                        kept_trial_idx, neural_trials, input_trials,\n                        output_trials, info, plot_dir)\n\n    info['t_total'] = time.time() - t0\n    return neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials, planes, info\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Diagnostic plots\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef plot_processing(sess, beh, tstart_inds, teleport_inds, dff, events,\n                    r_speed, keep_cells, matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    sid = info['session_id']\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(8, 1, figsize=(18, 26))\n\n    # window of ~6 trials to display\n    t0i, t1i = int(tstart_inds[0]), int(teleport_inds[min(5, len(teleport_inds) - 1)])\n    sl = slice(t0i, t1i)\n    tt = beh['time'][sl]\n\n    ax = axes[0]\n    cells = np.argsort(-np.nanmax(sess['F'], axis=1))[:3]\n    for c in cells:\n        ax.plot(tt, sess['F'][c, sl], lw=0.8, label=f'F cell {c}')\n        ax.plot(tt, sess['Fneu'][c, sl], lw=0.6, alpha=0.6, label=f'Fneu cell {c}')\n    ax.set_title(f'{sid}: step 1 - raw suite2p fluorescence (first ~6 trials)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('F (a.u.)')\n    ax.legend(fontsize=6, ncol=3)\n\n    ax = axes[1]\n    for c in cells:\n        ax.plot(tt, dff[c, sl], lw=0.8, label=f'dF/F cell {c}')\n    for s, e in zip(tstart_inds[:6], teleport_inds[:6]):\n        ax.axvspan(beh['time'][s], beh['time'][e], color='k', alpha=0.05)\n    ax.set_title('step 2 - dF/F (per-trial maximin baseline, neuropil subtracted, '\n                 'sigma=2 smoothing). Shading = trials; gaps = inter-trial intervals (NaN)')\n    ax.set_ylabel('dF/F')\n    ax.legend(fontsize=6, ncol=3)\n\n    ax = axes[2]\n    for c in cells:\n        ax.plot(tt, events[c, sl], ax.set_ylabel('events')\n    ax.legend(fontsize=6, ncol=3)\n\n    ax = axes[3]\n    ax.hist(r_speed[np.isfinite(r_speed)], bins=50)\n    ax.axvline(INT_R_THRESH, color='r')\n    ax.set_title(f'step 4 'task_description': (\n            'Head-fixed mice run down a 450 cm virtual linear track for water reward '\n            'delivered operantly for licking in a hidden 50 cm reward zone (zone A '\n            '80-130 cm, B 200-250 cm, C 320-370 cm; only one active at a time). Position/`t` linear-interpolated, `morph`/`trialnum`/`scanning` nearest-neighbour, `dz`/`lick`/`reward`/`tstart`/`teleport`/`rzone` cumulative-then-difference (so `lick`/`rzone` are *counts per imaging frame*), speed = smoothed d(pos)/dt |\n34\t| `dff` | `preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | ΔF/F: per-trial windows only, neuropil subtraction (`neu_coef=0.7`, neuropil trial-mean added back), `maximin` baseline (Gaussian σ=15 samples smoothing → `minimum_filter1d(300)` → `maximum_filter1d(300)`), dFF=(F−base)/|base|, then per-trial Gaussian σ=2 smoothing, then OASIS deconvolution (`dcnv.oasis(dff, 2000, tau, frame_rate/n_planes)`) → `events` |\n35\t| `multi_anim_sess` | `utilities.py` | PROCESSING/CURATION | Top-level per-day pipeline: calls `dff(..., neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin', neu_coef=0.7, deconvolve=True, keep_teleports=<per-animal>)`, then `get_trial_types`, `get_reward_zones`, `define_trial_subsets`, place-cell calc |\n36\t| `default_dff_method` | `utilities.py` | PROCESSING | `{neuropil_method_red:'subtract', baseline_method:'maximin', neu_coef:0.7, keep_teleports:False}` |\n37\t| `get_trial_types` | `behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Per trial (`trial_start_inds[i]:teleport_inds[i]`): `isreward = any(reward>0) AND any(rzone>0)`; `morph` = unique environment id |\n38\t| `get_reward_zones` | `behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Reward-zone [start,stop] and label ('A'/'B'/'C') per trial **from the scene name**; on `X_to_Y` scenes the zone changes at trial 30 (`change_trial=30`). Coordinates come from `reward_zone_dict` keys `X=[80,130] (A)`, `Y=[200,250] (B)`, `Z=[320,370] (C)` |\n39\t| `correct_lick_sensor_error` | `behavior.py` | CURATION | Trials where >`thr` fraction of frames have cumulative lick count >2 → licks set to NaN (sensor stuck). Paper: thr = 0.30, 81/12,376 trials |\n40\t| `is_putative_interneuron` | `spatial.py` | CURATION | Pearson r between each cell's dF/F and running speed over valid (within-trial) samples; `r > 0.5` ⇒ putative interneuron, excluded (`dayData.exclude_int=True`, `int_thresh=0.5`, `int_method='speed'`) |\n41\t| `teleport_metadata.teleport_sessions` | `teleport_metadata.py` | PROCESSING | Per-animal list of experiment days on which the laser was NOT blanked in the ITI ⇒ `keep_teleports=True` for ΔF/F baselining |\n42\t| `decode.CircularRegression` | `decode.py` | (paper analysis) | Paper's Fig-3 circular-linear decoder of reward-relative position from deconvolved events at speed >2 cm/s. Not used here (different decoder), but it confirms **deconvolved events** are the paper's neural signal of choice |\n43\t| `sessions_dict.single_plane/multi_plane` | `sessions_dict.py` | LOADING | Per-animal session metadata (date, scene, exp_day). Scene name ⇒ reward zone identity; reproduced in the NWB `identifier` field |\n44\t\n45\t### Notes\n46\t- Imaging: two-photon CA1, ~15.5 Hz **per plane**. m17/m18 are 2-plane (ETL); planes pooled for all analyses except Ext.Fig.7.\n47\t- Neural signal chain: raw `F`, `Fneu` (suite2p) → ΔF/F (reference `dff`) → OASIS `events`. The NWB file stores `Fluorescence` (raw F), `Neuropil` (Fneu) and `Deconvolved`. **The NWB `Deconvolved` series is suite2p's own `spks` (non-zero before the first trial start, i.e. computed over the whole session), NOT the paper's per-trial dFF-based `events`** — so ΔF/F + OASIS must be recomputed here, exactly as the paper does.\n48\t- Cell curation: suite2p `iscell` (manual curation, stored in the NWB `PlaneSegmentation`) then speed-correlation interneuron exclusion.\n49\t\n50\t---\n51\t\n52\t## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n53\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n54\t\n55\t### Data Structure\n56\t`/app/data/sub-<mouse>/sub-<mouse>_ses-<NN>_behavior+ophys.nwb`, one file per mouse-day\n57\t(`ses-NN` == experiment day, 1–14). Read with `pynwb.NWBHDF5IO`.\n58\t\n59\tContents used:\n60\t- `nwb.identifier` = original path, e.g. `/data/InVivoDA/GCAMP11/25_02_2023/Env1_LocationA_to_C` ⇒ **animal, date and scene name** (scene gives the reward-zone sequence).\n61\t- `nwb.subject.subject_id` = `m11`, …; `nwb.session_id` = experiment day.\n62\t- `processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries']`, one sample per imaging frame (`timestamps` = frame times, dt = 0.0644836 s = 1/15.5078125 Hz), series:\n63\t  `position` (cm; −500 before the VR/2P TTL sync), `speed` (cm/s, smoothed), `lick` (cumulative count per frame),\n64\t  `reward_zone` (count per frame of the VR reward-zone flag), `trial_start`, `teleport` (per-frame counts of the events),\n65\t  `trial number` (0-indexed; −1 before sync), `environment` (0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2; −1 before sync), `autoreward`, `scanning`,\n66\t  and `Reward` — a *sparse* series with one timestamp per delivered reward (0.004 mL).\n67\t- `processing['ophys']`: `Fluorescence/planeK` (n_frames × n_roi raw F), `Neuropil/planeK`, `Deconvolved/planeK` (suite2p spks — unused),\n68\t  `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation` with columns `pixel_mask`, `iscell` (n_roi × 2: [is-cell flag, probability]), `planeIdx`.\n69\t\n70\t### Dataset Size (from data files)\n71\t| Statistic | Value |\n72\t|-----------|-------|\n73\t| Subjects | 11 (m3, m4, m7, m11, m12, m13, m14, m15, m17, m18, m19) |\n74\t| Sessions | 152 (14/mouse; m11 has 12 — imaging started on day 3) |\n75\t| Sessions / subject | 12–14 |\n76\t| ROIs / session (all suite2p ROIs) | 315 – 4857 |\n77\t| `iscell`-curated neurons / session | 155 – 2341 (median ≈ 940) |\n78\t| Trials (total, = # `trial_start` flags) | 12,216 |\n79\t| Trials / session | mean 80.37, s.d. 6.14, range 41 – 100 |\n80\t| Imaging frames / session | 14,164 – 51,520 |\n81\t| Frame rate | 15.5078125 Hz/plane (dt = 64.484 ms) for every session |\n82\t| Frames inside trials (total) | ≈ 2.62 M |\n83\t| Trial duration | mean 13.8 s, median 12.3 s, range 6.2 – 216.6 s |\n84\t\n85\tNotes / edge cases found:\n86\t- `#trial_start` == `#teleport` in every session (no dangling trial).\n87\t- In 12 sessions (m17/m18, multi-plane) the ophys series has **exactly one more frame** than the behavior series\n88\t  (`vr_align_to_2P`'s documented \"one frame correction\"); ophys is truncated to the behavior length.\n89\t- Position at the `teleport` frame is a meaningless interpolation between end-of-track and the teleport zone\n90\t  (e.g. 350 cm when the previous sample is 446 cm) ⇒ the teleport sample must be excluded from the trial,\n91\t  which is exactly what the reference does (`licks[t_start:t_end]`, `f_[:, start-1:stop-1]`).\n92\t- `reward_zone` > 0 occurs only on trials where reward was actually available (~85% of trials).\n93\t\n94\t---\n95\t\n96\t## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n97\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n98\t\n99\t### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n100\t| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n101\t|-----------|-------|--------------|\n102\t| Mice (switch task) | 11 | \"Each mouse encountered a different starting reward zone … counterbalanced across mice (n = 11 mice)\" |\n103\t| Sessions | 14 days/mouse; m11 from day 3 | \"The task was imaged starting from day 1 for all mice except m11, for whom imaging started on day 3\" |\n104\t| Neurons / session | 155 – 2172 | \"This approach yielded 155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" |\n105\t| Trials / session | 80.5 ± 7.4 | \"mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials across 14 mice, all imaging days\" |\n106\t| Trials (total, switch mice) | 12,376 | \"n = 81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice\" |\n107\t| Lick-sensor-error trials | 81 (~0.65%) | \"~0.65% of all imaged trials, n = 81 out of 12,376 trials\" ; criterion \">30% of the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples in the trial containing a cumulative lick count >2\" |\n108\t| Reward omission rate | ~15% | \"the reward was randomly omitted on ~15% of trials\" |\n109\t| Putative interneurons excluded | 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells | \"Pearson correlation of >0.5 between their dF/F timeseries and the animal's running speed, excluding 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells\" |\n110\t| Neural time bin | 64.5 ms (15.5 Hz) | \"each frame is ~64.5 ms\"; \"~15.5 Hz\" |\n111\t| Track length | 450 cm | \"unidirectional 450 cm virtual linear track\" |\n112\t| Reward zones | A 80–130, B 200–250, C 320–370 cm | \"zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm\" |\n113\t| Reward-zone switch | after trial 30 | \"Each switch occurred after 30 trials\" |\n114\t| Environments | ENV1 / ENV2 | \"two virtual environments\"; `morph` 0/1 |\n115\t\n116\t### Processing Details\n117\t- ΔF/F: \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window … dF/F … fluorescence minus the baseline, divided by the absolute value of the baseline, then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\"\n118\t  (20 s × 15.5 Hz ≈ 310 samples ≈ the `int(300)` window in the code.)\n119\t- Per-trial limitation of the baseline \"accounts for potential photobleaching … and avoids the teleport periods for sessions during which the laser power was reduced\" ⇒ `keep_teleports` per animal/day (`teleport_metadata`).\n120\t- Spatial analyses exclude samples with speed < 2 cm/s (NOT applicable here — see Step 5 deviations).\n121\t- Temporal alignment for this conversion: **start of trial** (`trial_start`), per the Decoder Task spec.\n122\t\n123\t### Curation Steps\n124\t**Neuron curation rules**:\n125\t1. suite2p manual curation (`iscell[:,0] == 1`) — ROIs with multiple somata/dendrites, no visible transients, over-expression or interneuron-like continuous fluorescence were rejected.\n126\t2. Putative interneurons: Pearson r(dF/F, speed) > 0.5 over valid samples → excluded.\n127\t\n128\t**Trial curation rules**:\n129\t1. Trials with lick-sensor error (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) → licks unusable; the paper NaNs them. Here lick is a decoder **output**, so such trials are dropped.\n130\t2. Trial window = `[trial_start_ind, teleport_ind)` (the teleport sample itself is excluded, per reference code).\n131\t\n132\t### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n133\t| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n134\t|---|---|\n135\t| Reward-relative (circular) position from RR/TR/non-RR place-cell subsets (Fig. 3) | reported as a \"decode score\" = mean cos(y − ŷ) (1 = perfect, 0 = chance), ~0.5–0.8 for the example session, and as a z-score vs. circular-shift shuffle; no classification accuracies are reported |\n136\t\n137\tThe paper reports no categorical decoding accuracies, so there is no direct accuracy target for the\n138\tdecoder used here; the comparison target is the expert conversion.\n139\t\n140\t---\n141\t\n142\t## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n143\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n144\t\n145\t### Discrepancies Found\n146\t| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n147\t|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n148\t| Total trials | n/a | 12,216 `trial_start` flags over 152 sessions | \"12,376 trials across 11 switch mice\" | 1.3% fewer in the archive. `#trial_start == #teleport` in every session, so no trials are being missed by the parser; the paper's larger count most likely includes sessions not deposited on DANDI (e.g. the day-15/16/17 sessions that `teleport_metadata.py` lists for m15/m17/m18/m19, or the duplicate day-9 session commented out for GCAMP4 in `sessions_dict.py`). **Accepted**; everything derived from it (trials/session, lick-error count, reward rate) matches the paper. |\n149\t| Neurons / session | n/a | `iscell` count 155 – 2341 | \"155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" | The minimum matches **exactly** (155, m11 day 3). Only 3 of 152 sessions exceed 2172, all from m18 (2281, 2314, 2341), the mouse with the most ROIs and the only 2-plane animal with very dense segmentation. Most likely ROI re-curation between the paper analysis and the DANDI deposit. We use the `iscell` flags as archived, which is the only curation information in the released data. **Accepted, documented.** |\n150\t| Neural signal for the decoder | `multi_anim_sess` computes **both** `dff` and `events`; the paper's own decoder (`decode.py`, Fig. 3) uses `events`; peak/field/sequence analyses use `dff` (\"closest to the raw data\") | NWB ships raw `F`, `Fneu` and suite2p's own `Deconvolved` | ΔF/F and OASIS events both defined | Both were produced with the identical reference pipeline and both were tested (Step 7). **dF/F chosen** (see Step 5, Decision 2). |\n151\t| NWB `Deconvolved` series | reference `events` are NaN outside trials | NWB `Deconvolved` is non-zero before the first trial start, i.e. computed over the whole session from raw F | paper's events come from per-trial dF/F | The NWB `Deconvolved` is suite2p's own `spks`, **not** the paper's `events`. We recompute ΔF/F (and, optionally, OASIS) from `F`/`Fneu` exactly as the reference does. |\n152\t| Lick-error threshold | `correct_lick_sensor_error` default `correction_thr=0.5`; `lick_pos_std` passes 0.35 | thresholds 0.3/0.35/0.5 give 81/69/44 trials | \">30% of the … samples\"; \"n = 81 out of 12,376 trials\" | Used **0.30**, which reproduces the paper's count of **81** trials exactly. |\n153\t| Reward-zone coordinates | `reward_zone_dict` has both `'A':[175,225]` and `'X':[80,130]`; `get_reward_zones` maps Location A→`'X'`, B→`'Y'`, C→`'Z'` | first in-zone flag occurs at 80/200/320 cm | \"zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm\" | Used X/Y/Z = A/B/C = (80,130)/(200,250)/(320,370). Validated against the data: over all 10,394 trials with a reward-zone flag, the position at the first flagged frame is within 0–8.5 cm of the scene-derived zone start (mean +0.9 cm, the expected 1-frame lag at ~44 cm/s). Zero mismatches. |\n154\t| Speed threshold (2 cm/s) | applied for place-cell / decoder analyses in the paper | – | \"we excluded activity when the animal was moving at <2 cm s−1\" | **Not applied here**: speed is one of the decoder outputs (its lowest bin is exactly \"<2 cm/s\"), and dropping those samples would delete a whole output class and break the continuous per-trial time base required by the decoder task. Documented deviation, required by the Decoder Task spec. |\n155\t| Place-cell restriction | paper's Fig-3 decoder uses only RR / TR / non-RR **place cells** | – | – | **Not applied here**: that subsetting served a specific scientific comparison; a general-purpose decoder should see all curated pyramidal cells. Documented deviation. |\n156\t\n157\tEverything else (frame rate, trial windows, ΔF/F parameters, neuropil coefficient, interneuron\n158\tcriterion, switch trial 30, ENV1/ENV2 coding, ~15% omission) is consistent across the code, the\n159\tdata and the paper.\n160\t\n161\t---\n162\t\n163\t## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n164\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n165\t\n166\t### Variable Mapping\n167\t| Source (NWB) | Target field | Transform | Reference code | Notes |\n168\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n169\t| `ophys/Fluorescence/planeK` (raw F), `ophys/Neuropil/planeK` | `neural` | neuropil subtraction (0.7, trial-mean added back) → per-trial maximin baseline (σ=15 smoothing, 20 s min then max filter) → (F−base)/\\|base\\| → σ=2 Gaussian smoothing | `preprocessing.dff` called from `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | (n_neurons, T) float32 per trial |\n170\t| `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation['iscell'][:,0]` | neuron filter | keep == 1 | suite2p manual curation | |\n171\t| dF/F vs `behavior/speed` | neuron filter | drop cells with Pearson r > 0.5 | `spatial.is_putative_interneuron` | |\n172\t| `behavior/trial_start`, `behavior/teleport` | trial segmentation | trial = `[start_ind, teleport_ind)` | `behavior.get_trial_types`, `preprocessing.dff` | teleport sample excluded |\n173\t| `behavior/lick` | trial filter + `output[3]` | drop trial if >30% of frames have count > 2; else binarise (count > 0) | `behavior.correct_lick_sensor_error`; `behavior.lickrate` (`licks[licks>0]=1`) | |\n174\t| `behavior/position` timestamps | `input[0]` | t − t[trial_start] (s) | – | decoder-task requirement |\n175\t| `behavior/environment` (`morph`) | `input[1]` | constant per trial, 0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2 | `behavior.get_trial_types` | |\n176\t| `behavior/trial number` | `input[2]` | constant per trial, 0-indexed | – | |\n177\t| `behavior/Reward` + `behavior/reward_zone` | `input[3]`, `output[5]` | `isreward = any(reward) AND any(rzone)`; input is the **previous** trial's value | `behavior.get_trial_types` | first trial of a session ⇒ 0 |\n178\t| `behavior/position` + scene name | `output[0]` | signed distance to nearest point of the active reward zone → 7 bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | 0 while inside the zone |\n179\t| `behavior/position` | `output[1]` | 5 equal 90 cm bins over the 450 cm track | – | |\n180\t| `behavior/speed` | `output[2]` | 5 bins (<2, 2–10, 10–20, 20–40, >40 cm/s) | – | |\n181\t| scene name + trial index | `output[4]` | zone label A/B/C → 0/1/2, switching at trial 30 | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | |\n182\t| `nwb.subject.subject_id` | `subjects` / `subject_idx` | | | 11 mice |\n183\t| imaging plane / `ImagingPlane.location` | `brain_regions` / `brain_region_idx` | all \"CA1\" | paper: dorsal CA1 | per-neuron plane kept in `metadata.session_info[i].plane_idx` |\n184\t\n185\t### Key Decisions\n186\t1. **Temporal bins = imaging frames (64.484 ms)**. The VR behaviour in the NWB file has already been\n187\t   interpolated onto imaging frame times by the authors' `vr_align_to_2P`, so neural and behavioural\n188\t   streams are sample-for-sample aligned with no further resampling — the safest possible alignment.\n189\t   Every session is sampled at 15.5078125 Hz per plane, so the bin size is identical everywhere, as\n190\t   the target format requires. No additional binning was applied: it would only blur the licking and\n191\t   speed outputs.\n192\t2. **Neural signal = dF/F** (option `--neural-signal events` reproduces the deconvolved version).\n193\t   Both come from the identical reference pipeline. dF/F was chosen because (a) the paper treats it\n194\t   as the signal \"closest to the raw data\" and uses it for spatial-peak, field and sequence\n195\t   analyses; (b) OASIS deconvolution is explicitly *not* interpreted as a spike rate by the authors,\n196\t   it only removes the calcium kernel's asymmetry, which matters for spatial-tuning estimates rather\n197\t   than for per-frame decoding; and (c) it decodes better here — validation balanced accuracy on the\n198\t   2-session sample was higher for every one of the six outputs (e.g. position 0.625 vs 0.559,\n199\t   speed 0.553 vs 0.415, reward outcome 0.633 vs 0.568). Deconvolution discards sub-threshold\n200\t   fluctuations that a linear per-frame decoder can use.\n201\t3. **Trial window `[trial_start, teleport)`**, i.e. exactly the lap on the track. The reference\n202\t   `dff` uses `[start-1, stop-1)`, a 1-sample-shifted window; we use the un-shifted window so that\n203\t   the neural, input and output streams cover precisely the same frames and the trial contains no\n204\t   NaN. The teleport frame itself is excluded either way — its interpolated position is a meaningless\n205\t   blend of the end of the track and the teleport zone (e.g. 350 cm right after 446 cm).\n206\t4. **ΔF/F baseline windows follow `keep_teleports`** per animal/day from\n207\t   `teleport_metadata.teleport_sessions`: on sessions where the laser was not blanked, the baseline\n208\t   window for trial *i* starts one sample after the previous teleport (including the ITI), otherwise\n209\t   it is the trial itself. This is the reference behaviour and matters because a baseline taken over\n210\t   blanked (≈0) fluorescence would be meaningless.\n211\t5. **All outputs time-varying**. `reward_zone_location` and `reward_outcome` are constant within a\n212\t   trial but are broadcast over time, both because the format requires a single `doutput` for every\n213\t   trial and because the task asks for time-varying outputs wherever possible.\n214\t6. **Reward-zone identity from the scene name**, as the reference does, rather than from the\n215\t   `reward_zone` flag — the flag only fires on rewarded trials (~85%), so it cannot label omission\n216\t   trials. The scene-derived zone was validated against the flag on all trials where it does fire.\n217\t7. **`previous_trial_outcome` = 0 for the first trial** of each session (no preceding imaged trial).\n218\t   This affects 152/12,135 = 1.25% of trials; dropping those trials instead would discard real\n219\t   neural data for no benefit.\n220\t8. **Trials with lick-sensor error are dropped** (81 trials), rather than NaN-ed as in the paper,\n221\t   because `lick` is a decoder output and NaN is not a permitted value.\n222\t9. **No speed threshold and no place-cell restriction** (see Step 4).\n223\t\n224\t### Planned Sanity Checks\n225\t- [x] `#trial_start == #teleport` in every session; `teleport > trial_start` for every trial.\n226\t- [x] Scene-derived reward-zone start vs. the position at the first `reward_zone` flag (all trials, all sessions).\n227\t- [x] Lick-sensor-error trial count == 81 (paper).\n228\t- [x] Reward rate ≈ 85% (paper: ~15% omission).\n229\t- [x] Trials/session ≈ 80.5 ± 7.4 (paper).\n230\t- [x] Neurons/session range vs. the paper's 155–2172.\n231\t- [x] Putative-interneuron exclusion ≈ 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells (paper).\n232\t- [x] Re-derive neural/input/output values from the raw NWB with independent code and compare with `np.allclose` (Step 10, Check 2).\n233\t- [x] `morph` and `trial number` constant within every trial.\n234\t- [x] Reward-zone identity distribution ≈ uniform over A/B/C (counterbalanced design).\n235\t\n236\t---\n237\t\n238\t## Step 6: Script Development\n239\t**Status**: COMPLETE\n240\t\n241\t`/app/convert_data.py`. Structure:\n242\t- `nansmooth` — local port of `TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth` (TwoPUtils is not installed).\n243\t- `scene_reward_zones` — port of `behavior.get_reward_zones` for the scenes in this h5py fancy indexing per ROI would be very slow.\n255\t- Forking a process pool after importing suite2p aborts (\"fork() called from a process already\n256\t  using GNU OpenMP\").\n257\t\n258\tCode Gaussian kernel. The activity rate was extracted by deconvolving dF/F with a canonical calcium kernel using the OASIS algorithm as used in Suite2p.\"\n  (20 s × 15.5 Hz ≈ 310 samples ≈ the `int(300)` window in the code.)\n- Per-trial limitation of the baseline \"accounts for potential photobleaching … and avoids the teleport periods for sessions during which the laser power was reduced\" ⇒ `keep_teleports` per animal/day (`teleport_metadata`).\n- Spatial analyses exclude samples with speed < 2 cm/s (NOT applicable here — see Step 5 deviations).\n- Temporal alignment for this conversion: **start of trial** (`trial_start`), per the Decoder Task spec.\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. suite2p manual curation (`iscell[:,0] == 1`) — ROIs with multiple somata/dendrites, no visible transients, over-expression or interneuron-like continuous fluorescence were rejected.\n2. Putative interneurons: Pearson r(dF/F, speed) > 0.5 over valid samples → excluded.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Trials with lick-sensor error (>30% of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) → licks unusable; the paper NaNs them. Here lick is a decoder **output**, so such trials are dropped.\n2. Trial window = `[trial_start_ind, teleport_ind)` (the teleport sample itself is excluded, per reference code).\n\n### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Reward-relative (circular) position from RR/TR/non-RR place-cell subsets (Fig. 3) | reported as a \"decode score\" = mean cos(y − ŷ) (1 = perfect, 0 = chance), ~0.5–0.8 for the example session, and as a z-score vs. circular-shift shuffle; no classification accuracies are reported |\n\nThe paper reports no categorical decoding accuracies, so there is no direct accuracy target for the\ndecoder used here; the comparison target is the expert conversion.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Total trials | n/a | 12,216 `trial_start` flags over 152 sessions | \"12,376 trials across 11 switch mice\" | 1.3% fewer in the archive. `#trial_start == #teleport` in every session, so no trials are being missed by the parser; the paper's larger count most likely includes sessions not deposited on DANDI (e.g. the day-15/16/17 sessions that `teleport_metadata.py` lists for m15/m17/m18/m19, or the duplicate day-9 session commented out for GCAMP4 in `sessions_dict.py`). **Accepted**; everything derived from it (trials/session, lick-error count, reward rate) matches the paper. |\n| Neurons / session | n/a | `iscell` count 155 – 2341 | \"155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" | The minimum matches **exactly** (155, m11 day 3). Validated against the data: over all 10,394 trials with a reward-zone flag, the position at the first flagged frame is within 0–8.5 cm of the scene-derived zone start (mean +0.9 cm, the expected 1-frame lag at ~44 cm/s). Zero mismatches. |\n| Speed threshold (2 cm/s) | applied for place-cell / decoder analyses in the paper | – | \"we excluded activity when the animal was moving at <2 cm s−1\" | **Not applied here**: speed is one of the decoder outputs (its lowest bin is exactly \"<2 cm/s\"), and dropping those samples would delete a whole output class and break the continuous per-trial time base required by the decoder task. Documented deviation, required by the Decoder Task spec. |\n| Place-cell restriction | paper's Fig-3 decoder uses only RR / TR / non-RR **place cells** | – | – | **Not applied here**: that subsetting served a specific scientific comparison; a general-purpose decoder should see all curated pyramidal cells. Documented deviation. |\n\nEverything else (frame rate, trial windows, ΔF/F parameters, neuropil coefficient, interneuron\ncriterion, switch trial 30, ENV1/ENV2 coding, ~15% omission) is consistent across the code, the\ndata and the paper.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source (NWB) | Target field | Transform | Reference code | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `ophys/Fluorescence/planeK` (raw F), `ophys/Neuropil/planeK` | `neural` | neuropil subtraction (0.7, trial-mean added back) → per-trial maximin baseline (σ=15 smoothing, 20 s min then max filter) → (F−base)/\\|base\\| → σ=2 Gaussian smoothing | `preprocessing.dff` called from `utilities.multi_anim_sess` | (n_neurons, T) float32 per trial |\n| `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation['iscell'][:,0]` | neuron filter | keep == 1 | suite2p manual curation | |\n| dF/F vs `behavior/speed` | neuron filter | drop cells with Pearson r > 0.5 | `spatial.is_putative_interneuron` | |\n| `behavior/trial_start`, `behavior/teleport` | trial segmentation | trial = `[start_ind, teleport_ind)` | `behavior.get_trial_types`, `preprocessing.dff` | teleport sample excluded |\n| `behavior/lick` | trial filter + `output[3]` | drop trial if >30% of frames have count > 2; else binarise (count > 0) | `behavior.correct_lick_sensor_error`; `behavior.lickrate` (`licks[licks>0]=1`) | |\n| `behavior/position` timestamps | `input[0]` | t − t[trial_start] (s) | – | decoder-task requirement |\n| `behavior/environment` (`morph`) | `input[1]` | constant per trial, 0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2 | `behavior.get_trial_types` | |\n| `behavior/trial number` | `input[2]` | constant per trial, 0-indexed | – | |\n| `behavior/Reward` + `behavior/reward_zone` | `input[3]`, `output[5]` | `isreward = any(reward) AND any(rzone)`; input is the **previous** trial's value | `behavior.get_trial_types` | first trial of a session ⇒ 0 |\n| `behavior/position` + scene name | `output[0]` | signed distance to nearest point of the active reward zone → 7 bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | 0 while inside the zone |\n| `behavior/position` | `output[1]` | 5 equal 90 cm bins over the 450 cm track | – | |\n| `behavior/speed` | `output[2]` | 5 bins (<2, 2–10, 10–20, 20–40, >40 cm/s) | – | |\n| scene name + trial index | `output[4]` | zone label A/B/C → 0/1/2, switching at trial 30 | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | |\n| `nwb.subject.subject_id` | `subjects` / `subject_idx` | | | 11 mice |\n| imaging plane / `ImagingPlane.location` | `brain_regions` / `brain_region_idx` | all \"CA1\" | paper: dorsal CA1 | per-neuron plane kept in `metadata.session_info[i].plane_idx` |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Temporal bins = imaging frames (64.484 ms)**. The VR behaviour in the NWB file has already been\n   interpolated onto imaging frame times by the authors' `vr_align_to_2P`, so neural and behavioural\n   streams are sample-for-sample aligned with no further resampling — the safest possible alignment.\n   Every session is sampled at 15.5078125 Hz per plane, so the bin size is identical everywhere, as\n   the target format requires. No additional binning was applied: it would only blur the licking and\n   speed outputs.\n2. **Neural signal = dF/F** (option `--neural-signal events` reproduces the deconvolved version).\n   Both come from the identical reference pipeline. dF/F was chosen because (a) the paper treats it\n   as the signal \"closest to the raw data\" and uses it for spatial-peak, field and sequence\n   analyses; (b) OASIS deconvolution is explicitly *not* interpreted as a spike rate by the authors,\n   it only removes the calcium kernel's asymmetry, which matters for spatial-tuning estimates rather\n   than for per-frame decoding; and (c) it decodes better here — validation balanced accuracy on the\n   2-session sample was higher for every one of the six outputs (e.g. position 0.625 vs 0.559,\n   speed 0.553 vs 0.415, reward outcome 0.633 vs 0.568). Deconvolution discards sub-threshold\n   fluctuations that a linear per-frame decoder can use.\n3. **Trial window `[trial_start, teleport)`**, i.e. exactly the lap on the track. The reference\n   `dff` uses `[start-1, stop-1)`, a 1-sample-shifted window; we use the un-shifted window so that\n   the neural, input and output streams cover precisely the same frames and the trial contains no\n   NaN. The teleport frame itself is excluded either way — its interpolated position is a meaningless\n   blend of the end of the track and the teleport zone (e.g. 350 cm right after 446 cm).\n4. **ΔF/F baseline windows follow `keep_teleports`** per animal/day from\n   `teleport_metadata.teleport_sessions`: on sessions where the laser was not blanked, the baseline\n   window for trial *i* starts one sample after the previous teleport (including the ITI), otherwise\n   it is the trial itself. This is the reference behaviour and matters because a baseline taken over\n   blanked (≈0) fluorescence would be meaningless.\n5. **All outputs time-varying**. `reward_zone_location` and `reward_outcome` are constant within a\n   trial but are broadcast over time, both because the format requires a single `doutput` for every\n   trial and because the task asks for time-varying outputs wherever possible.\n6. **Reward-zone identity from the scene name**, as the reference does, rather than from the\n   `reward_zone` flag — the flag only fires on rewarded trials (~85%), so it cannot label omission\n   trials. The scene-derived zone was validated against the flag on all trials where it does fire.\n7. **`previous_trial_outcome` = 0 for the first trial** of each session (no preceding imaged trial).\n   This affects 152/12,135 = 1.25% of trials; dropping those trials instead would discard real\n   neural data for no benefit.\n8. **Trials with lick-sensor error are dropped** (81 trials), rather than NaN-ed as in the paper,\n   because `lick` is a decoder output and NaN is not a permitted value.\n9. **No speed threshold and no place-cell restriction** (see Step 4).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] `#trial_start == #teleport` in every session; `teleport > trial_start` for every trial.\n- [x] Scene-derived reward-zone start vs. the position at the first `reward_zone` flag (all trials, all sessions).\n- [x] Lick-sensor-error trial count == 81 (paper).\n- [x] Reward rate ≈ 85% (paper: ~15% omission).\n- [x] Trials/session ≈ 80.5 ± 7.4 (paper).\n- [x] Neurons/session range vs. the paper's 155–2172.\n- [x] Putative-interneuron exclusion ≈ 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells (paper).\n- [x] Re-derive neural/input/output values from the raw NWB with independent code and compare with `np.allclose` (Step 10, Check 2).\n- [x] `morph` and `trial number` constant within every trial.\n- [x] Reward-zone identity distribution ≈ uniform over A/B/C (counterbalanced design).\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py`. Structure:\n- `nansmooth` — local port of `TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth` (TwoPUtils is not installed).\n- `scene_reward_zones` — port of `behavior.get_reward_zones` for the scenes in this dataset.\n- `compute_dff_events` — port of `preprocessing.dff` (neuropil subtraction, maximin baseline,\n  smoothing, optional OASIS via `suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis`).\n- `speed_correlation` — vectorised `spatial.is_putative_interneuron(method='speed')`.\n- `discretize_*` — the three continuous→categorical maps from the Decoder Task spec.\n- `load_session` / `convert_session` — pynwb reading and per-trial assembly.\n- `plot_processing` — 8-panel per-session diagnostic figure (`--show-processing`).\n- `main` — process pool over sessions, assembly of the final dict, pickling.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Naive per-cell `np.corrcoef` loop for the interneuron test (O(n_cells) python loop).\n- Reading `F`/`Fneu` with h5py fancy indexing per ROI would be very slow.\n- Forking a process pool after importing suite2p aborts (\"fork() called from a process already\n  using GNU OpenMP\").\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Interneuron correlations vectorised into one matrix product (≈100× faster than the loop).\n- Whole `(n_frames, n_roi)` datasets read in one contiguous call, then column-masked and transposed.\n- OASIS deconvolution skipped entirely when the neural signal is dF/F.\n- `ProcessPoolExecutor` with a **spawn** context, 12 workers.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\n(sessions m11 day 4, a \"stay\" session, and m13 day 3, a reward-switch session).","numLines":270,"startLine":1,"totalLines":611}}}

Four of six are 2.6–4.3× chance. Two need comment:\n513\t\n514\t- **lick, 0.768 (1.54× chance)** — a binary output, so 1.54× chance is 77% balanced accuracy, i.e.\n515\t  the decoder identifies licking frames well. Binary outputs cannot exceed 2× chance, so the\n516\t  multiplier is not a meaningful yardstick here.\n517\t- **reward_outcome, 0.602 (1.20× chance)** — investigated in detail below; this is a ceiling\n518\t  imposed by the task, not a conversion defect.\n519\t\n520\t**reward_outcome diagnostic** (`/app/cache/rew_diag.py`, 26 sessions spanning all 11 mice): balanced\n521\taccuracy split by where the animal is in the trial —\n522\t\n523\t| Trial phase | n (validation frames) | Train bal. acc. | Validation bal. acc. |\n524\t|---|---|---|---|\n525\t| before the reward zone (distance bin < 3) | 33,638 | 0.925 | **0.531** |\n526\t| inside the reward zone (bin == 3) | 20,997 | 0.965 | **0.613** |\n527\t| after the reward zone (bin > 3) | 33,300 | 0.958 | **0.653** |\n528\t\n529\tValidation accuracy is at chance *before* the animal reaches the reward zone and rises monotonically\n530\tthrough and after it. That is exactly the causal structure of the task: whether this trial is\n531\trewarded is simply not determined — and certainly not observable in CA1 — until the animal licks in\n532\tthe zone. Because the task specification requires the per-trial outcome to be broadcast over the\n533\twhole trial, roughly half of the frames carry no information at all, capping the achievable overall\n534\taccuracy near 0.6–0.7. No change to the conversion can lift the pre-zone frames above chance.\n535\t\n536\t### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the paper\n537\t| Variable | This conversion (validation balanced acc.) | Paper |\n538\t|---|---|---|\n539\t| Reward-relative position (`distance_to_reward_zone`) | 0.621 (7 classes, chance 0.143) | Fig. 3: circular-linear decoder, \"decode score\" = mean cos(y−ŷ), ~0.5–0.8 in the example session and z-scored against a circular-shift shuffle — **not an accuracy and not comparable numerically** |\n540\t| Position, speed, lick, reward zone, reward outcome | 0.761 / 0.628 / 0.768 / 0.873 / 0.602 | **not decoded in the paper** |\n541\t\n542\tThe paper reports no categorical decoding accuracies for any variable (its only decoder is the\n543\tcircular-linear reward-relative position model of Fig. 3, scored with a cosine similarity over\n544\tcontinuous circular position and restricted to place-cell subpopulations at speeds > 2 cm/s), so\n545\tthere is no published number that can be beaten or missed. As a qualitative comparison, the paper's\n546\tcentral claim — that CA1 carries a strong code for position relative to reward — is reproduced here:\n547\tdistance-to-reward is decoded at 4.3× chance, the highest chance-multiple of all six outputs, and\n548\thigher than absolute position relative to its own chance level (3.8×).\n549\t\n550\t### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n551\t| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio |\n552\t|---|---|---|---|\n553\t| distance_to_reward_zone | 0.798 | 0.621 | 1.29 |\n554\t| position | 0.891 | 0.761 | 1.17 |\n555\t| speed | 0.732 | 0.628 | 1.17 |\n556\t| lick | 0.796 | 0.768 | 1.04 |\n557\t| reward_zone_location | 0.963 | 0.873 | 1.10 |\n558\t| reward_outcome | 0.933 | 0.602 | **1.55** |\n559\t\n560\tOnly `reward_outcome` exceeds 1.5×. The diagnostic above localises it: training accuracy is 0.93\n561\t*even before the reward zone*, where validation accuracy is 0.53. The decoder is memorising\n562\ttrial-specific neural fluctuations — each session has its own 100-PC projection fitted to ~80 trials,\n563\tand reward outcome is constant within a trial, so a per-trial \"fingerprint\" fits the training labels\n564\tperfectly. This is an over-fitting property of a per-trial constant label under this architecture,\n565\tnot data leakage: the train/test split is by trial, each trial's frames go entirely to one side of\n566\tthe split, and no output value is present in the input (the only outcome variable among the inputs\n567\tis the *previous* trial's outcome, which the decoder legitimately receives and which is uninformative\n568\tabout the current trial — confirmed by the at-chance pre-zone validation accuracy).\n569\t\n570\t### Additional debugging performed (per the checklist)\n571\t1. Fig-3 decoder uses `events` | `dff` (default), `events` available via `--neural-signal events` | ⚠️ deliberate (Step 5, Decision 2) |\n\nEvery deviation is listed above with its reason; there are no unintentional differences.\n\n### Check 4 — Key statistics comparison\nSee the Step 9 table. Matches: subjects (11), sessions (152), trials/session (79.8 ± 6.9 vs 80.5 ± 7.4),\nlick-error trials (81, exact), reward omission (~15%), minimum neurons/session (155, exact),\ninterneuron exclusion (0.32 ± 0.60% vs 0.42 ± 0.85%), frame rate (64.484 ms), reward-zone\ncoordinates, switch trial 30. Two residual differences (total trials 12,135 vs 12,376; maximum\nneurons/session 2341 vs 2172 in 3 of 152 sessions) are investigated and explained in Step 4; both\nconcern data that is simply not in the DANDI deposit, and neither is fixable from the released files.\n\n### Check 5 — Edge cases (script run over all 152 files)\n| Edge case | Finding | Handling |\n|---|---|---|\n| dangling / overlapping trials | 0 sessions with `trial_start[i] <= teleport[i-1]`; `#starts == #teleports` everywhere | assertions in `convert_session` |\n| multiple events in one frame | `trial_start`/`teleport` counts never exceed 1 | `np.where(>0)` is exact |\n| ophys longer than behaviour | 10 sessions have exactly one extra imaging frame | ophys truncated to the behaviour length (the reference's \"one frame correction\") |\n| teleport-sample position artefact | position at the teleport frame is an interpolation between ~446 cm and the teleport zone | teleport sample excluded from the trial |\n| position slightly out of [0, 450] | within-trial range is −2.74 … 451.83 cm | binning clips into bins 0 and 4 |\n| negative speeds | within-trial speed range −6.45 … 133.1 cm/s (brief backwards drift) | falls in the \"<2 cm/s\" bin, as intended |\n| NaNs in behaviour | none in position/speed/lick over all sessions | still guarded: trials with non-finite values would be dropped and counted (`n_dropped_nan` = 0 everywhere) |\n| first trial of a session | no preceding trial | `previous_trial_outcome = 0`, documented |\n| shortest trial | 96 frames (6.2 s) | no minimum-length filter needed |\n| sessions with < 2 trials | none (min 40 kept trials) | decoder requires ≥ 2 trials/session — satisfied |\n| `keep_teleports` window underflow | `teleport[i-1]+1 > trial_start[i]` never occurs | `min(...)` guard retained anyway |\n| multi-plane animals | m17/m18 have 2 `planeK` series and a shared `PlaneSegmentation` | planes pooled (as in the paper); per-neuron plane kept in metadata |\n| ROI/plane index mismatch | `PlaneSegmentation` holds all planes' ROIs concatenated | `iscell` masked per plane via `planeIdx` before matching the plane's F columns; verified by the independent sanity check |\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\n- **Process pool aborted** (\"fork() called from a process already using GNU OpenMP\") because suite2p\n  initialises OpenMP at import. Fixed by using a `spawn` multiprocessing context.\n- **Neural signal choice**: the first full-pipeline test used deconvolved events; replaced by dF/F\n  after the comparison in Step 8 (Step 5, Decision 2).\n- No other issues were found; all checks above pass on the final `converted_data.pkl`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 11: Full Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/train_decoder.py /app/converted_data.pkl --plot-samples`\n(152 sessions, 12,135 trials, 138,298 neurons, 2.58 M timepoints; ~13 min on the L4 GPU).\n\n### Training Progress\n- Loss decreasing: **Yes**, monotonically, 3.338 → 0.651 over 200 epochs (no plateau or divergence).\n\n### Decoder Results (Full)\n| Output | #classes | Chance | Training balanced acc. | Validation balanced acc. | Val / chance |\n|--------|----------|--------|------------------------|--------------------------|--------------|\n| distance_to_reward_zone | 7 | 0.143 | 0.798 | **0.621** | 4.3× |\n| position | 5 | 0.200 | 0.891 | **0.761** | 3.8× |\n| speed | 5 | 0.200 | 0.732 | **0.628** | 3.1× |\n| lick | 2 | 0.500 | 0.796 | **0.768** | 1.54× |\n| reward_zone_location | 3 | 0.333 | 0.963 | **0.873** | 2.6× |\n| reward_outcome | 2 | 0.500 | 0.933 | **0.602** | 1.20× |\n\n`predictions.png` shows the decoded traces (dashed) following the true traces (solid) for position,\ndistance-to-reward, speed and reward-zone identity essentially step for step.\n\n---\n\n## Step 12: Critical Review 2\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Check 1 — Accuracy vs chance\nNo output is at or below chance. Four of six are 2.6–4.3× chance. Two need comment:\n\n- **lick, 0.768 (1.54× chance)** — a binary output, so 1.54× chance is 77% balanced accuracy, i.e.\n  the decoder identifies licking frames well. Binary outputs cannot exceed 2× chance, so the\n  multiplier is not a meaningful yardstick here.\n- **reward_outcome, 0.602 (1.20× chance)** — investigated in detail below; this is a ceiling\n  imposed by the task, not a conversion defect.\n\n**reward_outcome diagnostic** (`/app/cache/rew_diag.py`, 26 sessions spanning all 11 mice): balanced\naccuracy split by where the animal is in the trial —\n\n| Trial phase | n (validation frames) | Train bal. acc. | Validation bal. acc. |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| before the reward zone (distance bin < 3) | 33,638 | 0.925 | **0.531** |\n| inside the reward zone (bin == 3) | 20,997 | 0.965 | **0.613** |\n| after the reward zone (bin > 3) | 33,300 | 0.958 | **0.653** |\n\nValidation accuracy is at chance *before* the animal reaches the reward zone and rises monotonically\nthrough and after it. That is exactly the causal structure of the task: whether this trial is\nrewarded is simply not determined — and certainly not observable in CA1 — until the animal licks in\nthe zone. Because the task specification requires the per-trial outcome to be broadcast over the\nwhole trial, roughly half of the frames carry no information at all, capping the achievable overall\naccuracy near 0.6–0.7. No change to the conversion can lift the pre-zone frames above chance.\n\n### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the paper\n| Variable | This conversion (validation balanced acc.) | Paper |\n|---|---|---|\n| Reward-relative position (`distance_to_reward_zone`) | 0.621 (7 classes, chance 0.143) | Fig. 3: circular-linear decoder, \"decode score\" = mean cos(y−ŷ), ~0.5–0.8 in the example session and z-scored against a circular-shift shuffle — **not an accuracy and not comparable numerically** |\n| Position, speed, lick, reward zone, reward outcome | 0.761 / 0.628 / 0.768 / 0.873 / 0.602 | **not decoded in the paper** |\n\nThe paper reports no categorical decoding accuracies for any variable (its only decoder is the\ncircular-linear reward-relative position model of Fig. 3, scored with a cosine similarity over\ncontinuous circular position and restricted to place-cell subpopulations at speeds > 2 cm/s), so\nthere is no published number that can be beaten or missed. As a qualitative comparison, the paper's\ncentral claim — that CA1 carries a strong code for position relative to reward — is reproduced here:\ndistance-to-reward is decoded at 4.3× chance, the highest chance-multiple of all six outputs, and\nhigher than absolute position relative to its own chance level (3.8×).\n\n### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Validation | Ratio |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| distance_to_reward_zone | 0.798 | 0.621 | 1.29 |\n| position | 0.891 | 0.761 | 1.17 |\n| speed | 0.732 | 0.628 | 1.17 |\n| lick | 0.796 | 0.768 | 1.04 |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.963 | 0.873 | 1.10 |\n| reward_outcome | 0.933 | 0.602 | **1.55** |\n\nOnly `reward_outcome` exceeds 1.5×. The diagnostic above localises it: training accuracy is 0.93\n*even before the reward zone*, where validation accuracy is 0.53. The decoder is memorising\ntrial-specific neural fluctuations — each session has its own 100-PC projection fitted to ~80 trials,\nand reward outcome is constant within a trial, so a per-trial \"fingerprint\" fits the training labels\nperfectly. This is an over-fitting property of a per-trial constant label under this architecture,\nnot data leakage: the train/test split is by trial, each trial's frames go entirely to one side of\nthe split, and no output value is present in the input (the only outcome variable among the inputs\nis the *previous* trial's outcome, which the decoder legitimately receives and which is uninformative\nabout the current trial — confirmed by the at-chance pre-zone validation accuracy).\n\n### Additional debugging performed (per the checklist)\n1. Output values verified against the raw NWB for 45 randomly chosen trials across 9 sessions,\n   all 6 outputs × all timepoints, exact match (Step 10, Check 2).\n2. Temporal alignment verified by plotting neural + behaviour + converted outputs for the same\n   trials (`processing_*.png`, panels 5–8): the `time_from_trial_start` input is a clean saw-tooth\n   resetting exactly at trial boundaries, the position staircase resets with it, and the\n   distance-to-reward trace is flat at 0 precisely while the animal is inside the reward zone.\n3. Output variation checked: no output is degenerate — the most imbalanced is `reward_outcome`\n   at 84/16, and balanced accuracy (with `balanced_loss=True`) is used throughout.\n4. Neural filtering verified: `iscell` + interneuron exclusion reproduce the paper's per-session\n   neuron range and its 0.42 ± 0.85% interneuron rate.\n5. Processing verified against the reference function by function (Step 10, Check 3) and numerically\n   against an independent re-implementation (Step 10, Check 2).\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\n- None outstanding. The single flagged metric (`reward_outcome`) is explained by task structure and\n  quantified above; no conversion change can improve it.\n\n---\n\n## Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- [x] `README.md` created (dataset description, loading instructions, format spec, key statistics)\n- [x] `cache/` folder created with the investigation scripts and `README_CACHE.md`\n- [x] All files organised\n\n### Final file inventory\n| File | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `convert_data.py` | the conversion script |\n| `converted_data.pkl` | full converted dataset (9.63 GB) |\n| `sample_data.pkl` | 2-session sample |\n| `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` | this document |\n| `README.md` | user-facing documentation |\n| `conversion_sample_out.txt`, `conversion_full_out.txt` | conversion logs |\n| `verification_sample_out.txt`, `verification_full_out.txt` | format-verification logs |\n| `train_decoder_sample_out.txt`, `train_decoder_full_out.txt` | decoder training logs |\n| `processing_m11_day04.png`, `processing_m13_day03.png` | per-step processing diagnostics |\n| `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png` | decoder sample trials and predictions |","numLines":340,"startLine":270,"totalLines":611}}}
